-modules (resp. finitely presented étale
\varphi
-modules, finite projective étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules, finitely presented
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules) over
R
.

For
n\geq 1
, let
W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\coloneq W(\mathbb{F}_{q})/p^{n}=\mathcal{O}_{K}/p^{n}
. Let
S
be an affine
W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
-scheme. Suppose that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\text{(i) the affine scheme}\ S\ \text{is flat over}\ W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q}),\\
\text{(ii) the special fibre}\ S\times_{W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\mathbb{F}_{q}\ \text{is normal and integral},\\
\text{(iii) there is an endomorphism}\ \varphi_{q}\ \text{of}\ S\ \text{which induces the}\ q\text{-th power map on}\ S\times\mathbb{F}_{q}\end{cases}

(4.2)

For example,
S\coloneq\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n})
,
\mathrm{Spec}(\varphi_{q})
satisfies (
4.2
): its special fibre is
\mathrm{Spec}A_{q}
, which is a normal integral scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
by
[
BHHMS23
, Corollary 3.1.1.2]
and
[
Mat89
, Theorem 19.4]
, and
S
is flat over
W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
since
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}
is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
.

For a scheme
(S,\varphi_{q})
satisfying (
4.2
, (iii)), we denote by
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
the category of pairs
(\mathcal{V},\phi_{\mathcal{V}})
where
\mathcal{V}
is a Zariski-locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module of finite rank with an isomorphism
\phi_{\mathcal{V}}:\varphi_{q}^{*}\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{V}
of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules. Fix a geometric point
\overline{s}
of
S
, we denote by
\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s}))
the category of finite free
W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
-modules with a continuous
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s})
-action, where
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s})
is the étale fundamental group of
(S,\overline{s})
. For the definition and basic properties of
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s})
, see
[
Ray71
, Exposé V]
.

Proposition 4.4
(Katz)

Let
(S,\varphi_{q})
be a scheme satisfying (
4.2
). Then there is an equivalence of categories between
\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s}))
and
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
. The functor is given as follows: for an object
\rho_{n}
of
\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(S,\overline{s}))
,
\rho_{n}
corresponds to an étale local system of
W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
-modules
\mathcal{F}_{n}
over
S
, then
(\nu_{*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\mathcal{O}_{S}),\nu_{*}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}))
is an object of
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
, where
\nu:S_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow S_{\mathrm{Zar}}
is the restriction from the étale topos to the Zariski topos.

Proof
.

This is a
[
Kat73
, Proposition 4.1.1]
.

Proposition 4.5

Let
R
be a perfect
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra, and suppose that
R
is an integral domain. For every
n\geq 1
, let
W_{n}(R)\coloneq W(R)/p^{n}
, and let
\varphi
be the unique endomorphism of
W_{n}(R)
such that
\overline{\varphi}:R\rightarrow R
is the
q
-th power map. Then there is a natural equivalence between the category
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi}(W_{n}(R))
and the category
\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(R),\overline{s}))
, where
\overline{s}
is a fixed geometric point of
\mathrm{Spec}(R)
.

Proof
.

By
[
KL15
, Proposition 3.2.7]
, there is a natural equivalence between the category
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi}(W_{n}(R))
and the category of étale shaves of flat
W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})/p^{n}
-modules over
\mathrm{Spec}(R)
represented by finite étale
R
-schemes. Since
R
is an integral domain, there is an equivalence of categories between the latter category and
\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(R),\overline{s}))
(see for example
[
Sta24
,
Tag 03RV
]
).

Proposition 4.6

For
n\geq 1
, the functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(W(A_{q,\infty})/p^{n})

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow W(A_{q,\infty})/p^{n}\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}}M

is an equivalence of categories, where
A_{q,\infty}
is the completed perfection of
A_{q}
.

Proof
.

There is a morphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q,\infty})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}).

(4.3)

We fix a geometric point
\overline{x}_{\infty}
of
\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q,\infty})
, and denote the image of
\overline{x}
in
\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q})
(resp.
\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n})
) by
\overline{x}
(resp.
\overline{x}^{\prime}
). Recall that an
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}
-module
M
is locally free if and only if
M
is finite projective, hence there is an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}),-):\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n})})\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}).

(4.4)

Combining with Proposition
4.4
, there is an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\Big(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}),\overline{x}^{\prime})\Big)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}).

(4.5)

By Proposition
4.5
, there is an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\Big(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q,\infty}),\overline{x}_{\infty})\Big)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(W(A_{q,\infty})/p^{n}).

(4.6)

Note that (
4.3
) induces a natural map of profinite groups

\displaystyle\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q,\infty}),\overline{x}_{\infty})\rightarrow\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q}),\overline{x})\rightarrow\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}),\overline{x}^{\prime}).

(4.7)

The map
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q}),\overline{x})\rightarrow\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}),\overline{x}^{\prime})
is an isomorphism since it is induced by a universal homeomorphism of schemes (
[
AGV06
, EXPOSÉ VIII, Théorème 1.1]
), and
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q,\infty}),\overline{x}_{\infty})\rightarrow\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q}),\overline{x})
is also an isomorphism since
\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q,\infty})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q})
factorizes as

\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q,\infty})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q}^{\mathrm{perf}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q})

where the first morphism induces an isomorphism of étale fundamental groups (
[
SW20
, Theorem 7.4.8]
) and the second morphism is a universal homeomorphism of schemes. Therefore, the composition (
4.7
) is an isomorphism.

Combining with (
4.5
) and (
4.6
), there is a commutative diagram of categories:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}),\overline{x}^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{W_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Spec}(A_{q,\infty}),\overline{x}_{\infty}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(W(A_{q,\infty})/p^{n}).}
pullback
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(A_{q,\infty})/p^{n}\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}}-}

Then the conclusion follows.

Theorem 4.7

The functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},E})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}M

is an equivalence of categories.

Proof
.

First we assume that
E=K
. For fully faithfulness, let
M,N
be finite projective étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}
,
M_{\infty}\coloneq W(A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}M
,
N_{\infty}\coloneq W(A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}N
. If
f:M\rightarrow N
is a homomorphism of
\varphi_{q}
-modules, and
f_{\infty}\coloneq\mathrm{Id}\otimes f:M_{\infty}\rightarrow N_{\infty}
is the zero map, then the map
\overline{f}_{\infty}:M_{\infty}/p^{n}\rightarrow N_{\infty}/p^{n}
induced by
f_{\infty}
is zero. By Proposition
4.6
, we deduce that, for any
n\geq 1
, the map
\overline{f}:M/p^{n}\rightarrow N/p^{n}
induced by
f
is zero, hence
f=0
. Moreover, let
h_{\infty}:M_{\infty}\rightarrow N_{\infty}
be a homomorphism of
\varphi_{q}
-modules, by Proposition
4.6
, for every
n
, there exists a unique homomorphism
h_{n}:M/p^{n}\rightarrow N/p^{n}
of
\varphi_{q}
-modules such that
\mathrm{Id}\otimes h_{n}:M_{\infty}/p^{n}\rightarrow N_{\infty}/p^{n}
is induced by
h_{\infty}
. Then
\{h_{n}\}_{n}
induces a homomorphism
h:M\rightarrow N
of
\varphi_{q}
-modules, and
\mathrm{Id}\otimes h=h_{\infty}
.

For the essential surjectivity, let
M_{\infty}
be a finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
W(A_{q,\infty})
of rank
r
, by Proposition
4.6
, for every
n
, there exists a finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
M_{n}
of rank
r
over
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}
such that
M_{\infty}/p^{n}\cong W(A_{q,\infty})/p^{n}\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}}M_{n}
. Moreover, these
M_{n}
are compatible, i.e.
M_{n+1}/p^{n}\cong M_{n}
as finite projective étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}
. We put
M\coloneq\varprojlim_{n}M_{n}
, which is a
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}
. Take
x_{1},\dots,x_{r}\in M
such that
\overline{x_{1}},\dots,\overline{x_{r}}
forms an
A_{q}
-basis of
M_{1}\cong M/p
, then
\overline{x_{1}},\dots,\overline{x_{r}}
forms a
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}
-basis of
M_{n}\cong M/p^{n}
for any
n
. As
p
is contained in the Jacobson radical of
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}
, using Nakayama’s lemma, we deduce that
x_{1},\dots,x_{r}
generate
M
as an
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}
-module. Moreover, if
\sum_{i=1}^{r}a_{i}x_{i}=0
, by reducing modulo
p^{n}
for
n\geq 1
, we get
a_{i}\in\bigcap_{n\geq 1}p^{n}A_{\mathrm{mv},K}=0
for any
0\leq i\leq r
, thus
M=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}A_{\mathrm{mv},K}x_{i}
is free, and we have

A_{\mathrm{mv},K}\otimes_{\varphi_{q},A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}M\cong\varprojlim_{n}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K}\otimes_{\varphi_{q},A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}M_{n})\xrightarrow[\sim]{\varprojlim_{n}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi_{q})}\varprojlim_{n}M_{n}=M,

hence
M
is a finite projective étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}
. Moreover,

M_{\infty}\cong\varprojlim_{n}\left(W(A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}/p^{n}}M_{n}\right)\cong W(A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}\varprojlim_{n}M_{n}=W(A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}M,

which proves the essential surjectivity.

Now we show the fully faithfulness for the general case. The faithfulness can be deduced directly from the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},E})}(M_{1},M_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}))}(M_{1,\infty},M_{2,\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K})}(M_{1},M_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(W(A_{q,\infty}))}(M_{1,\infty},M_{2,\infty})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

(4.8)

where
M_{1},M_{2}
are finite projective étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
,
M_{i,\infty}\coloneq W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}M_{i}
for
i=1,2
, and in the bottom horizontal map, we use the identification

W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}M_{i}\cong W(A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}M_{i},\quad i=1,2.

For
f_{\infty}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}))}(M_{1,\infty},M_{2,\infty})
, the commutative diagram (
4.8
) shows that
f_{\infty}=\mathrm{id}\otimes f
for some
f\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},K})}(M_{1},M_{2})
. Since
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}=\mathcal{O}_{E}\otimes_{\sigma_{0},\mathcal{O}_{K}}A_{\mathrm{mv},K}
, if we can show that
f
is
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-linear, then
f
is
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
-linear, which finishes the proof of the fully faithfulness. For
x\in\mathcal{O}_{E}
, consider the map

\displaystyle f_{x}:M_{1}\rightarrow M_{2},\ m_{1}\mapstochar\rightarrow f(x\cdot m_{1})-x\cdot f(m_{1}).

Then
f_{x}
is a homomorphism of
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}
. Moreover, for any
a\otimes m_{1}\in W(A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}M_{1}\cong M_{1,\infty}
,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id}\otimes f_{x})(a\otimes m_{1})=a\otimes f(xm_{1})-a\otimes(xf(m_{1}))

Using the identification
W(A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}M_{1}\cong M_{1,\infty}
, since
\mathrm{id}\otimes f_{x}=f_{\infty}
is
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-linear, we have

\displaystyle a\otimes f(xm_{1})=f_{\infty}(a\otimes xm_{1})

\displaystyle=f_{\infty}(x\cdot(a\otimes m_{1}))

\displaystyle=x\cdot f_{\infty}(a\otimes m_{1})=x\cdot(a\otimes f(m_{1}))=a\otimes(xf(m_{1})).

Thus
\mathrm{id}\otimes f_{x}=0
, hence
f_{x}=0
by the fully faithfulness of
W(A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}-
, i.e.
f
is
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-linear.

It remains to show the essential surjectivity.
Let
M_{\infty}
be a finite projective étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
. The case
E=K
implies that there is a finite projective étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
M
over
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}
such that

W(A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}M\cong M_{\infty}.

For
a\in\mathcal{O}_{E}
, the scalar multiplication
M_{\infty}\rightarrow M_{\infty},m\mapstochar\rightarrow a\cdot m
(viewing
M_{\infty}
as a
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
-module) is an endomorphism of the étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
M_{\infty}
over
W(A_{q,\infty})
, hence descends to an endomorphism
g_{a}
of the étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
M
over
A_{\mathrm{mv},K}
, which gives an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-module structure on
M
. Moreover, if
a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
, then the scalar multiplication by
a
on
M
agrees with
g_{\sigma_{0}(a)}
, since they are the same on
M_{\infty}
. Hence there is a
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}=\mathcal{O}_{E}\otimes_{\sigma_{0},\mathcal{O}_{K}}A_{\mathrm{mv},K}
-module structure on
M
. Since

W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}M\cong W(A_{q,\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}M\cong M_{\infty}

is finite projective over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
and
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}\rightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty})
is faithfully flat (Proposition
3.9
), by faithfully flat descent of projectivity (see for example
[
Sta24
,
Tag 058S
]
),
M
is a finite projective module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
. Hence by Lemma
4.1
,
M
is a finite free
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
-module. Besides, it is easy to check that
M
is a
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
. Since

A_{\mathrm{mv},E}\otimes_{\varphi_{q},A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}M\cong A_{\mathrm{mv},K}\otimes_{\varphi_{q},A_{\mathrm{mv},K}}M\cong M,

we see that
M
is a finite projective étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
.

4.2
Finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules

In this section, we use a standard lemma in algebraic
K
-theory (Lemma
4.12
) to extend Theorem
4.7
to finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
, then we add the
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
-action to deduce that every finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
descends.

Let
R
be a ring,
M
be an
R
-module,
r\in R
, we put
M[r]\coloneq\left\{m\in M:rm=0\right\}
which is a submodule of
M
. We start with the structure of finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
.

Lemma 4.8

If
I\subseteq A
is an ideal of
A
, and
A\cdot\varphi_{q}(I)=I
, then
I=0
or
I=A
.

Proof
.

Note that
\varphi_{q}^{f^{\prime}}=\mathrm{Frob}_{q^{\prime}}:A\rightarrow A
, where
q^{\prime}=q^{f^{\prime}}=\#\mathbb{F}
. In particular,

A\cdot\varphi_{q}^{nf^{\prime}}(I)\subseteq I^{q^{\prime n}},\quad n\geq 1.

Since
A\cdot\varphi_{q}(I)=I
, by induction on
m\geq 1
, we deduce
A\cdot\varphi_{q}^{m}(I)=I
for any
m\geq 1
. As a consequence, we have

I=A\cdot\varphi_{q}^{nf^{\prime}}(I)\subseteq I^{q^{\prime n}}\subseteq I,\quad n\geq 1,

which implies that
I^{q^{\prime n}}=I
for any
n\geq 1
. Therefore,

I=\bigcap_{n=1}^{+\infty}I^{q^{\prime n}}\subseteq\bigcap_{n=1}^{+\infty}I^{n}.

On the other hand,
A
is a Noetherian domain, hence if
I
is a proper ideal of
A
, by Krull’s intersection theorem,
\bigcap_{n=1}I^{n}=0
, hence
I=0
.

Corollary 4.9

If
M
is a finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A
, then
M
is a finite free
A
-module.

Proof
.

Let
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(M)
be the
k
-th fitting ideal of
M
,
k\geq 0
(for the definition, see
[
Sta24
,
Tag 07Z8
]
). By
[
Sta24
,
Tag 07ZA
]
, we have

\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(M)=\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(A\otimes_{\varphi_{q},A}M)=A\cdot\varphi_{q}(\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(M)).

Then Lemma
4.8
implies that
\mathrm{Fit}_{k}(M)
is either
0
or
A
. Thus
[
Sta24
,
Tag 07ZD
]
implies that
M
is a finite projective
A
-module, hence is finite free by
[
L“”ut77
,
\lx@sectionsign
1 satz 3]
(Lemma
4.1
).

Proposition 4.10

Let
M
be a non-zero finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
. Then there exists
1\leq n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}\leq\infty
and
m_{1},\dots,m_{r}\geq 1
such that

M\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\left(A_{\mathrm{mv},E}/\varpi^{n_{i}}\right)^{\oplus m_{i}},

here we use the convention that
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}/\varpi^{\infty}=A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
.

Proof
.

As
\varphi_{q}:A_{\mathrm{mv},E}\rightarrow A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
is faithfully flat, we can deduce that
\varpi^{s}M/\varpi^{s+1}M
is a finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A
for any
s\geq 0
, hence is finite free (Corollary
4.9
).
We apply
[
Mar24
, Theorem 4.3]
to deduce that

M\cong M_{\infty}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r-1}M_{i}

with
M_{\infty}
is a finite projective
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
-module, and
M_{i}
is a finite projective
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}/\varpi^{n_{i}}
-module for some
1\leq n_{i}<+\infty
. By Lemma
4.1
, every
M_{i}
is a finite free
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}/\varpi^{n_{i}}
-module, and
M_{\infty}
is a finite free
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
-module.

Since
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
is a Noetherian domain, and
\varphi_{q}:A_{\mathrm{mv},E}\rightarrow A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
is flat (Lemma
2.1
and Lemma
2.3
), one can easily deduce that

Lemma 4.11

The categories
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},E})
and
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},E})
are abelian categories.

We quote an important and technical lemma:

Lemma 4.12

Let
R
be a ring with an endomorphism
\varphi_{q}
. Let
M
be a finitely generated étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
R
. Then there exists a finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
F
and a
\varphi_{q}
-equivariant surjection
F\twoheadrightarrow M
.

Proof
.

This is
[
KL15
, Lemma 1.5.2]
.

Corollary 4.13

Let
R
be a ring with a flat endomorphism
\varphi_{q}
. Let
M
be a finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
R
. Then there exists an exact sequence
F^{\prime}\rightarrow F\rightarrow M\rightarrow 0
of étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
R
, where
F,F^{\prime}
are finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
R
.

Proof
.

By Lemma
4.12
, there exists an exact sequence
F\xrightarrow{f}M\rightarrow 0
of étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
R
. Since
M
is finitely presented,
\ker f
is finitely generated. Since
M
,
F
are étale and
\varphi_{q}
is flat,
\ker f
is étale, then we apply Lemma
4.12
to
\ker f
to conclude.

Let
M_{\infty}
be a finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
. By Lemma
4.12
, we can take a finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
F_{\infty}
over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
and a
\varphi_{q}
-equivariant surjection
f_{\infty}:F_{\infty}\twoheadrightarrow M_{\infty}
. By Theorem
4.7
, there exists a unique finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
F
over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
such that
F_{\infty}\cong W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F
. We use the injection
F\hookrightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F,x\mapstochar\rightarrow 1\otimes x
to identify
F
with a
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
-submodule of
F_{\infty}
, then we put

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})\coloneq f_{\infty}(F)\subset M_{\infty}.

(4.9)

Then
\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})
is a finite
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
-module with an endomorphism
\varphi_{q}
induced by the endomorphism of
F
.

Suppose that there is another finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
F_{\infty}^{\prime}
over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
and a
\varphi_{q}
-equivariant homomorphism
f_{\infty}^{\prime}:F_{\infty}^{\prime}\rightarrow M_{\infty}
(not necessarily surjective). Let
F^{\prime}
be a finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
such that
F_{\infty}^{\prime}\cong W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F^{\prime}
, we have the following lemma:

Lemma 4.14

With notations above, we have
f_{\infty}^{\prime}(F^{\prime})\subseteq f_{\infty}(F)
.

Proof
.

Let
L
be the fibre product of the diagram
F_{\infty}^{\prime}\rightarrow M_{\infty}\twoheadleftarrow F_{\infty}
in the category of
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
-modules,

L=\left\{(x,x^{\prime})\in F_{\infty}\oplus F_{\infty}^{\prime}:f_{\infty}(x)=f_{\infty}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\right\},

then
L
is a
\varphi_{q}
-module, and by the surjectivity of
f_{\infty}
, there is a short exact sequence of
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
:

0\rightarrow\ker f_{\infty}\xrightarrow{x\mapstochar\rightarrow(x,0)}L\xrightarrow{(x,x^{\prime})\mapstochar\rightarrow x^{\prime}}F_{\infty}^{\prime}\rightarrow 0.

As
M_{\infty}
is finitely presented,
\ker f_{\infty}
must be a finite
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
-module, hence
L
is a finite
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
-module. Moreover,
M_{\infty}
and
F_{\infty}
are finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules, hence a standard argument using the five lemma implies that
\ker f_{\infty}
is étale, hence
L
is étale. Therefore, we can apply Lemma
4.12
to deduce that there exists a finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
F_{\infty}^{\prime\prime}
with a
\varphi_{q}
-equivariant surjection
F_{\infty}^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow L
. Let
h_{1,\infty}
be the composition
F_{\infty}^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow L\rightarrow F_{\infty}
, and let
h_{2,\infty}
be the composition
F_{\infty}^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow L\twoheadrightarrow F_{\infty}^{\prime}
, there is a commutative diagram of finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\infty}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\infty}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\infty}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{2,\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1,\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\infty}^{\prime}}

Let
F^{\prime\prime}
be the finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
such that
F^{\prime\prime}_{\infty}\cong W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes F^{\prime\prime}
, then
h_{1,\infty}
and
h_{2,\infty}
descend to
F^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{h_{1}}F
and
F^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{h_{2}}F^{\prime}
(Theorem
4.7
). Moreover, by Proposition
3.9
,
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}\hookrightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty})
is faithfully flat, hence
h_{2}
is surjective. Therefore,

f_{\infty}^{\prime}(F^{\prime})=f_{\infty}^{\prime}(h_{2,\infty}(F^{\prime\prime}))=f_{\infty}(h_{1,\infty}(F^{\prime\prime}))\subseteq f_{\infty}(F).

In particular,
\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})
is independent of the choice of
F_{\infty}
and the surjection
f_{\infty}:F_{\infty}\twoheadrightarrow M_{\infty}
.

Definition 4.15

Let
M_{\infty}
be a finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
. We call
\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})
the
decompletion and deperfection
of
M_{\infty}
. Moreover, any homomorphism
h_{\infty}:M_{\infty}\rightarrow N_{\infty}
between finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
restricts to a homomorphism
h:\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})\rightarrow\mathrm{Dec}(N_{\infty})
of finite
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
-modules which commutes with
\varphi_{q}
. Thus
\mathrm{Dec}
is a functor sending finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
to finite
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
-modules with a semi-linear endomorphism
\varphi_{q}
.

Lemma 4.16

Let
f:F^{\prime}\rightarrow F
be a homomorphism of finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
which induces a homomorphism
f_{\infty}:W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F^{\prime}\rightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F
of étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
. Then

F\cap\mathrm{Im}(f_{\infty})=\mathrm{Im}(f).

Proof
.

Obviously,
\mathrm{Im}(f)\subseteq F\cap\mathrm{Im}(f_{\infty})
. We put
F^{\prime}_{\infty}\coloneq W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes F^{\prime}
and
F_{\infty}\coloneq W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes F
. Let
e_{1}^{\prime},\dots,e_{r}^{\prime}
be an
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
-basis of
F^{\prime}
, then
\mathrm{Im}(f)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}A_{\mathrm{mv},E}f(e_{i}^{\prime})
, and
\mathrm{Im}(f_{\infty})=\sum_{i=1}^{r}W_{E}(A_{\infty})f(e_{i}^{\prime})
. As a consequence,
\mathrm{Im}(f_{\infty})
is the image of the natural map

\displaystyle W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}\mathrm{Im}(f)\rightarrow F_{\infty}.

(4.10)

On the other hand,
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}\rightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty})
is flat, hence (
4.10
) is also injective as it is the base change of
\mathrm{Im}(f)\hookrightarrow F
, hence we may identify
\mathrm{Im}(f_{\infty})
with
W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}\mathrm{Im}(f)
. Now consider the following commutative diagram with exact rows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Im}(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F/\mathrm{Im}(f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Im}(f_{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\infty}/\mathrm{Im}(f_{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\infty}}

Note that
F_{\infty}/\mathrm{Im}(f_{\infty})\cong W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F/\mathrm{Im}(f)
, and by
[
Sta24
,
Tag 05CK
]
and Lemma
3.9
, the right vertical map is injective. Hence if
x\in F\cap\mathrm{Im}(f_{\infty})
, we have
\pi_{\infty}(x)=0
, which implies that
\pi(x)=0
, hence
x
lies in
\mathrm{Im}(f)
. Therefore,
\mathrm{Im}(f)=F\cap\mathrm{Im}(f_{\infty})
.

As a consequence, we can deduce the following result:

Proposition 4.17

For any finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
M_{\infty}
over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
,
\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})
is a finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
.

Proof
.

We only need to show that the linearization map (
4.1
)
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}\otimes_{\varphi_{q}}\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})\rightarrow\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})
is an isomorphism.
By Corollary
4.13
, there exist a short exact sequence of étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules

F_{\infty}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g_{\infty}}F_{\infty}\xrightarrow{f_{\infty}}M_{\infty}\rightarrow 0,

where
F_{\infty},F_{\infty}^{\prime}
are finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
, and this induces a sequence of finite
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
by Theorem
4.7
and Definition
4.15
:

\displaystyle F^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}F\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})\rightarrow 0,

(4.11)

where
F,F^{\prime}
are finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
such that
F_{\infty}\cong W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F
,
F_{\infty}^{\prime}\cong W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F^{\prime}
.
By (
4.9
),
f
is surjective and
f\circ g=0
. Moreover, if
x\in F
,
f(x)=0
, then
x\in F\cap\mathrm{Im}(g_{\infty})=\mathrm{Im}(g)
by Lemma
4.16
. Thus (
4.11
) is an exact sequence. Then the étaleness of
F^{\prime}
and
F
implies that
\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})
is étale. Hence
\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})
is a finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
.

Theorem 4.18

The functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dec}:\ \mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},E})

\displaystyle M_{\infty}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})

is an equivalence of categories with a quasi-inverse given by
W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}-
. Moreover,
\mathrm{Dec}
preserves short exact sequences.

Proof
.

We need to show that there are natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dec}\circ\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}-\right)\cong\mathrm{Id},W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}\left(\mathrm{Dec}(-)\right)\cong\mathrm{Id}.

For a finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
M
over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
, by Lemma
4.12
, there exists a
\varphi_{q}
-equivariant surjection
f:F\twoheadrightarrow M
, where
F
is a finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
. Then applying the functor
W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}-
, we get a
\varphi_{q}
-equivariant surjection
f_{\infty}:W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F\twoheadrightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}M
. By Definition
4.15
, we have

\mathrm{Dec}\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}M\right)=f_{\infty}(F)=M.

Conversely, for a finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
M_{\infty}
over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
, By the proof of Proposition
4.17
, there is an exact sequence of étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules
F_{\infty}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g_{\infty}}F_{\infty}\xrightarrow{f_{\infty}}M_{\infty}\rightarrow 0
, where
F_{\infty}^{\prime}
,
F_{\infty}
are as in
loc. cit.
, and the functor
\mathrm{Dec}
induces an exact sequence
F^{\prime}\xrightarrow{g}F\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})\rightarrow 0
. Then there is a commutative diagram with exact rows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\infty}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\otimes g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\otimes f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\infty}}

where the right vertical map is the natural homomorphism

W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})\rightarrow M_{\infty},\ x\otimes m\mapstochar\rightarrow xm.

This implies that the natural homomorphism
W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})\rightarrow M_{\infty}
is an isomorphism.

It remains to show that
\mathrm{Dec}
preserves short exact sequences, which is a direct consequence of the fact that
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}\hookrightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty})
is faithfully flat (Lemma
3.9
) and
\mathrm{Dec}
is a quasi-inverse of
W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}-
.

Now we add the action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
.

Corollary 4.19

The functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dec}:\ \mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}(A_{\mathrm{mv},E})

\displaystyle M_{\infty}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})

is an equivalence of categories with a quasi-inverse given by
W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}-
. Moreover, the functor
\mathrm{Dec}
preserves short exact sequences

Proof
.

Let
M_{\infty}
be a finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
. Let
a\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
, since
W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{a,W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M_{\infty}
is also a finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
, by Theorem
4.18
, the natural isomorphism

W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{a,W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M_{\infty}\xrightarrow{\sim}M_{\infty},\ x\otimes m\mapstochar\rightarrow xa(m)

descends to an isomorphism of
\varphi_{q}
-modules

\mathrm{Dec}\Big(W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{a,W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M_{\infty}\Big)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty}).

Let
f_{\infty}:F_{\infty}\twoheadrightarrow M_{\infty}
be a surjection of finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules, where
F_{\infty}=W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F
for some finite free étale
\varphi_{q}
-module
F
over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
. Then we get a surjection

\displaystyle W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}\left(A_{\mathrm{mv},E}\otimes_{a,A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}F\right)

\displaystyle\cong W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{a,W_{E}(A_{\infty})}F_{\infty}{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\mathrm{id}\otimes f_{\infty}}}W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{a,W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M_{\infty},

which easily implies that

\mathrm{Dec}\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{a,W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M_{\infty}\right)\cong A_{\mathrm{mv},E}\otimes_{a,A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty}),

hence we get an endomorphism

a:\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})\xrightarrow{m\mapstochar\rightarrow 1\otimes m}A_{\mathrm{mv},E}\otimes_{a,A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})\cong\mathrm{Dec}(W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{a}M_{\infty})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty}).

This gives the semi-linear action of
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}
on
\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})
which makes
\mathrm{Dec}(M_{\infty})
into a finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
. By the same argument in the proof of Theorem
4.18
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Dec}\circ\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}-\right)\cong\mathrm{Id},\ W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{mv},E}}\left(\mathrm{Dec}(-)\right)\cong\mathrm{Id},

which finishes the proof.

When
K=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we have
\mathbb{F}(\negthinspace(T_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\negthinspace)=A_{\infty}
and
A_{\mathrm{cycl},E}=A_{\mathrm{mv},E}
, hence

Lemma 4.20

The functor

\displaystyle W_{E}\left(\mathbb{F}(\negthinspace(T_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\negthinspace)\right)\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{cycl},E}}-:\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\left(A_{\mathrm{cycl},E}\right)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\left(W_{E}\left(\mathbb{F}(\negthinspace(T_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\negthinspace)\right)\right)

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow W_{E}\left(\mathbb{F}(\negthinspace(T_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{1/p^{\infty}})\negthinspace)\right)\otimes_{A_{\mathrm{cycl},E}}M

is an equivalence of categories.

As a consequence of Proposition
4.10
and Theorem
4.18
, we have

Proposition 4.21

Let
M
be a non-zero finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
. Then there exists
1\leq n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}\leq\infty
and
m_{1},\dots,m_{r}\geq 1
such that

M\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n_{i}}\right)^{\oplus m_{i}},

here we use the convention that
W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{\infty}=W_{E}(A_{\infty})
.

5
Descent from
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
to
W_{E}(A_{\infty})

The goal of this section is to show that every finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
canonically descends to a finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
.

5.1
A vanishing result on certain continuous cohomology groups

Throughout this section, for a topological space
X
and a topological ring
R
, we denote by
C(X,R)
the set of continuous maps from
X
to
R
.

Recall that the perfectoid ring
A_{\infty}^{\prime}\coloneq\mathbb{F}\left(\negthinspace\left(T_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{1/p^{\infty}}\right)\negthinspace\right)\left\langle\left(\frac{T_{\mathrm{LT},i}}{T_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{p^{i}}}\right)^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}:1\leq i\leq f-1\right\rangle
admits an
\mathbb{F}
-linear continuous
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-action (see
[
BHHMS23a
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.3 (28),
\lx@sectionsign
2.5 (47)]
, where the variable
T_{K,i}
of
loc. cit.
is denoted by
T_{\mathrm{LT},i}
here) given by

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\varphi_{q}(T_{\mathrm{LT},i})=T_{\mathrm{LT},i}^{q},&0\leq i\leq f-1\\
\underline{a}(T_{\mathrm{LT},i})=a_{i,\mathrm{LT}}(T_{\mathrm{LT},i}),&\underline{a}=(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})\in(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f},0\leq i\leq f-1.\end{cases}

(5.1)

The topology on
A_{\infty}^{\prime}
is defined by the multiplicative norm
|\cdot|:A_{\infty}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
sending
T_{\mathrm{LT},i}
to
p^{-1/p^{i}}
.

Let
M
be a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-module over
A_{\infty}^{\prime}
, and let
\Delta_{1}\coloneq\{(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})\in\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}:\prod_{i}a_{i}=1\}
be a subgroup of
\left(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}\right)^{f}
. In this section, we prove that

H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},M)=0,k\geq 1.

We recall the definition of continuous cohomology groups. Let
G
be a topological group, and let
M
be a topological abelian group with a continuous
G
-action. The
i
-th
continuous cohomology group

H^{i}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,M)
is computed by the complex of abelian groups

C_{\mathrm{cont}}^{0}(G,M)\xrightarrow{d^{0}}C_{\mathrm{cont}}^{1}(G,M)\xrightarrow{d^{1}}C_{\mathrm{cont}}^{2}(G,M)\rightarrow\cdots

where
C_{\mathrm{cont}}^{0}(G,M)=M
,
C_{\mathrm{cont}}^{i}(G,M)=C(G^{i},M)
for
i\geq 1
,
d^{0}:M\rightarrow C_{\mathrm{cont}}^{1}(G,M)
is the map sending
m
to the function
g_{1}\mapstochar\rightarrow g_{1}(m)-m
and for
i\geq 1

\displaystyle d^{i}(f)(g_{1},\dots,g_{i+1}):=g_{1}\cdot f(g_{2},\dots,g_{i+1})

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{i}(-1)^{j}f(g_{1},\dots,g_{j}g_{j+1},\dots,g_{i+1})

(5.2)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{i+1}f(g_{1},\dots,g_{i}).

Lemma 5.1

Let
G
be a profinite group. Suppose that
0\rightarrow M^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}}M\xrightarrow{f^{\prime\prime}}M^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow 0
is an exact sequence of continuous
G
-modules. If
f^{\prime\prime}
admits local sections, i.e., for every element
m^{\prime\prime}
of
M^{\prime\prime}
, there exists an open neighbourhood
U
of
m^{\prime\prime}
in
M^{\prime\prime}
and a continuous map
s:U\rightarrow M
such that
f^{\prime\prime}\circ s=\mathrm{id}_{U}
, then there is a long exact sequence of abelian groups

0\rightarrow M^{\prime G}\rightarrow M^{G}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow H^{i-1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,M^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow H^{i}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,M^{\prime})\rightarrow H^{i}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,M)\rightarrow H^{i}_{\mathrm{cont}}(G,M^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow\cdots.

Proof
.

This is a direct consequence of
[
Lic09
, Proposition 1.3, Corollary 2.4]
.

Recall the following theorem in
[
BHHMS23a
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.5 Theorem 2.5.1]
:

Theorem 5.2

The functor
A_{\infty}^{\prime}\otimes_{m}-:\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}(A_{\infty})\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q},\left(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}\right)^{f}}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
is an equivalence of categories which is exact and preserves the rank. A quasi-inverse is given by
\left(-\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
.

Let
M
be a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},\left(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}\right)^{f})
-module over
A_{\infty}^{\prime}
of rank
r
. By Theorem
5.2
, as a
\Delta_{1}
-module, it is isomorphic to
\left(A_{\infty}^{\prime}\right)^{\oplus r}
. Therefore, for any
m\geq 1
, there is an isomorphism of abelian groups

H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},M)\cong H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},A_{\infty}^{\prime})^{\oplus r},\quad k\geq 1.

Thus to show
H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},M)=0
, it suffices to show:

Theorem 5.3

For
k\geq 1
, we have

H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},A_{\infty}^{\prime})=0.

Proof
.

Let
Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\coloneq\mathrm{Spa}(A_{\infty},A_{\infty}^{\circ})
and
U_{\underline{n}_{0}}\coloneq\mathrm{Spa}(A_{\infty}^{\prime},\left(A_{\infty}^{\prime}\right)^{\circ})
which are affinoid perfectoid spaces. By
[
BHHMS23a
, Proposition 2.4.4]
, there is a morphism
U_{\underline{n}_{0}}\xrightarrow{m}Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
which is a pro-étale
\Delta_{1}
-torsor. In particular, it is a covering in the pro-étale site
\left(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\right)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
. We also denote the ring homomorphism
A_{\infty}\hookrightarrow A_{\infty}^{\prime}
by
m
.

Recall that for any perfectoid space
X
and any profinite group
G
, the fibre product
\underline{G}\times X
of pro-étale sheaves is represented by the perfectoid space
X\times G\coloneq\varprojlim\limits_{H=\text{ open subgroups of }G}X\times G/H
, where
\underline{G}
is the pro-étale sheaf sending
Y
to the set of continuous maps from
Y
to
G
, and
X\times G/H
is a finite disjoint union of
G/H
copies of
X
(
[
SW20
,
\lx@sectionsign
9.3]
,
[
Sch22
, Lemma 10.13]
). In particular, for
n\geq 1
we have the perfectoid space
U_{\underline{n_{0}}}\times\Delta_{1}^{n}
. In fact, we have

\displaystyle U_{\underline{n_{0}}}\times\Delta_{1}^{n}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Spa}\left(C(\Delta_{1}^{n},A_{\infty}^{\prime}),C(\Delta_{1}^{n},\left(A_{\infty}^{\prime}\right)^{\circ})\right),\quad n\geq 1.

(5.3)

Claim: for
n\geq 1
, there is an isomorphism of affinoid perfectoid spaces.

\displaystyle U_{\underline{n_{0}}}\times\Delta_{1}^{n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times(n+1)}

\displaystyle\left(x,g_{1},\dots,g_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow\left(x,g_{1}(x),\dots,\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{i}\right)(x),\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\right)(x)\right),

where
U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times(n+1)}\coloneq U_{\underline{n_{0}}}\times_{Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}}U_{\underline{n_{0}}}\times_{Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}}\cdots U_{\underline{n_{0}}}
is the fibre product of
n+1
copies of
U_{\underline{n_{0}}}
for
n\geq 0
. We shall prove the claim by induction on
n\geq 1
. The case
n=1
is proved by the first paragraph of the proof of
[
Wei17
, Proposition 4.3.2]
. For
n\geq 2
, then there is an isomorphism of perfectoid spaces

U_{\underline{n_{0}}}\times\Delta_{1}^{n}\cong\left(U_{\underline{n_{0}}}\times\Delta_{1}^{n-1}\right)\times\Delta_{1}\cong U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times n}\times\Delta_{1}\cong U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times(n-1)}\times_{Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}}\left(U_{\underline{n_{0}}}\times\Delta_{1}\right)\cong U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times(n+1)},

which proves the claim. As a consequence, using (
5.3
), for
n\geq 1
, there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle t^{n}:\mathcal{O}_{U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times(n+1)}}\left(U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times(n+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}C(\Delta_{1}^{n},A_{\infty}^{\prime})

(5.4)

\displaystyle a_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow\left((g_{1},\dots,g_{n})\mapstochar\rightarrow a_{0}\cdot g_{1}(a_{1})\cdot(g_{1}g_{2})(a_{2})\cdot\cdots\cdot\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\right)(a_{n})\right),

where we use the isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times(n+1)}}(U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times(n+1)})\cong A_{\infty}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{A_{\infty}}\cdots\widehat{\otimes}_{A_{\infty}}A_{\infty}^{\prime}\eqcolon\widehat{\otimes}^{n+1}_{A_{\infty}}A_{\infty}^{\prime},\quad n\geq 1.

We let
t^{0}
be the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times 1}}(U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times 1})=\mathcal{O}_{U_{\underline{n_{0}}}}(U_{\underline{n_{0}}})\cong A_{\infty}^{\prime}\eqcolon\widehat{\otimes}^{1}_{A_{\infty}}A_{\infty}^{\prime}
.

By
[
SW20
, Proposition 8.2.8]
, the presheaf
\mathcal{O}:X\mapstochar\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}(X)
is a sheaf on
\left(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\right)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, and for
i\geq 1

H^{i}\left(\left(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\right)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{O}\right)=0
. Since
U_{\underline{n_{0}}}\rightarrow Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is a pro-étale covering, by
[
Sta24
,
Tag 03OW
]
, there is a spectral sequence

E_{2}^{k,l}=\check{H}^{k}\left(\mathcal{U},\underline{H}^{l}(\mathcal{O})\right)\Longrightarrow H^{k+l}\left(\left(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\right)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{O}\right),

where
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{\underline{n_{0}}}\rightarrow Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\}
,
\underline{H}^{l}(\mathcal{O})
is the pre-sheaf sending
X
to
H^{l}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{O})
on
\left(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\right)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, and
\check{H}^{k}\left(\mathcal{U},\underline{H}^{l}(\mathcal{O})\right)
is the
k
-th Čech cohomology group associated to the covering
\mathcal{U}
and the presheaf
\underline{H}^{l}(\mathcal{O})
. Since
U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times n}
is affinoid perfectoid for every
n\geq 1
, by
[
SW20
, Proposition 8.2.8]
, we have

\underline{H}^{l}(\mathcal{O})(U_{\underline{n}_{0}}^{\times n})=H^{l}\left(\left(U_{\underline{n}_{0}}^{\times n}\right)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{O}\right)=0,\quad l\geq 1

hence

\check{H}^{k}\left(\mathcal{U},\underline{H}^{l}(\mathcal{O})\right)=0,\quad l\geq 1,k\geq 0.

Therefore, the spectral sequence collapses at the second page, and induces an isomorphism

\check{H}^{k}\left(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}\right)\cong H^{k}\left(\left(Z_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{\mathrm{gen}}\right)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{O}\right)=0,\quad k\geq 1.

On the other hand, by the definition of Čech cohomology groups,
\check{H}^{k}\left(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}\right)
is computed by the complex

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times 1}}(U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times 1})\xrightarrow{\partial^{0}}\mathcal{O}_{U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times 2}}(U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times 2})\xrightarrow{\partial^{1}}\mathcal{O}_{U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times 3}}(U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times 3})\xrightarrow{\partial^{2}}\cdots.

(5.5)

To be explicit, for
n\geq 0
, using the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times(n+1)}}(U_{\underline{n_{0}}}^{\times(n+1)})\cong\widehat{\otimes}^{n+1}_{A_{\infty}}A_{\infty}^{\prime}
,
\partial^{n}
is given by

\displaystyle\partial^{n}:\quad\widehat{\otimes}^{n+1}_{A_{\infty}}A_{\infty}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\widehat{\otimes}^{n+2}_{A_{\infty}}A_{\infty}^{\prime}

\displaystyle a_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow\sum_{i=0}^{n+1}(-1)^{i}a_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{i-1}\otimes 1\otimes a_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}.

Then for
n\geq 0
,
a_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}\in\widehat{\otimes}^{n+1}_{A_{\infty}}A_{\infty}^{\prime}
and
(g_{1},\dots,g_{n+1})\in\Delta_{1}^{n+1}
, using (
5.2
), (
5.4
) and (
5.5
), we have

\displaystyle d^{n}\Big(t^{n}(a_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})\Big)\big(g_{1},\dots,g_{n+1}\big)

\displaystyle=g_{1}\Big(a_{0}\cdot g_{2}(a_{1})\cdot(g_{2}g_{3})(a_{2})\cdot\cdots\cdot\left(g_{2}\cdots g_{n+1}\right)(a_{n})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{n}(-1)^{l}a_{0}\cdot g_{1}(a_{1})\cdot\cdots\cdot(g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{i-1})(a_{i-1})

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot(g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{i}g_{i+1})(a_{i})\cdot\cdots\cdot(g_{1}\cdots g_{n+1})(a_{n})

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{n+1}a_{0}\cdot g_{1}(a_{1})\cdot\cdots\cdot\left(g_{1}\cdots g_{n}\right)(a_{n})

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=0}^{n}\left(\left(\prod_{j=1}^{i+1}g_{j}\right)(a_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{n}(-1)^{l}a_{0}\prod_{i=1}^{l-1}\left(\left(\prod_{j=1}^{i}g_{j}\right)(a_{i})\right)\prod_{i=l}^{n}\left(\left(\prod_{j=1}^{i+1}g_{j}\right)(a_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{n+1}a_{0}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\left(\prod_{j=1}^{i}g_{j}\right)(a_{i})\right)

and

\displaystyle t^{n+1}\Big(\partial^{n}(a_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})\Big)\big(g_{1},\dots,g_{n+1}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{n+1}(-1)^{l}t^{n+1}\left(a_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes 1\otimes a_{l}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}\right)(g_{1},\dots,g_{n+1})

\displaystyle=t^{n+1}\left(1\otimes a_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}\right)(g_{1},\dots,g_{n+1})

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{n}(-1)^{l}t^{n+1}\left(a_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes 1\otimes a_{l}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}\right)(g_{1},\dots,g_{n+1})

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{n+1}t^{n+1}\left(a_{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}\otimes 1\right)(g_{1},\dots,g_{n+1})

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=0}^{n}\left(\left(\prod_{j=1}^{i+1}g_{j}\right)(a_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=1}^{n}(-1)^{l}a_{0}\prod_{i=1}^{l-1}\left(\left(\prod_{j=1}^{i}g_{j}\right)(a_{i})\right)\prod_{i=l}^{n}\left(\left(\prod_{j=1}^{i+1}g_{j}\right)(a_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(-1)^{n+1}a_{0}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\left(\prod_{j=1}^{i}g_{j}\right)(a_{i})\right).

We see that
t^{n+1}\circ\partial^{n}=d^{n}\circ t^{n}
, hence there is a commutative diagram of complexes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\otimes}^{n}_{A_{\infty}}A_{\infty}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\otimes}^{n+1}_{A_{\infty}}A_{\infty}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\otimes}^{n+2}_{A_{\infty}}A_{\infty}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathrm{cont}}^{n-1}(\Delta_{1},A_{\infty}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathrm{cont}}^{n}(\Delta_{1},A_{\infty}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathrm{cont}}^{n+1}(\Delta_{1},A_{\infty}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{n+1}}

Thus
H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},A_{\infty}^{\prime})\cong\check{H}^{k}\left(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}\right)=0
for
k\geq 1
.

Corollary 5.4

Let
M
be a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},\left(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}\right)^{f})
-module over
A_{\infty}^{\prime}
. Then for
k\geq 1
,

H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},M)=0.

As
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
, we equip
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\coloneq\mathcal{O}_{E}\otimes_{W(\mathbb{F})}W(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
with the weak topology so that the
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-action on
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
is continuous. The ring homomorphism
m:A_{\infty}\hookrightarrow A_{\infty}^{\prime}
lifts to a map
m:W_{E}(A_{\infty})\hookrightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
commuting with the continuous
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-actions, where
(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f}
acts on
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
via the map
(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times},(a_{0},\dots,a_{f-1})\mapstochar\rightarrow\prod_{i=0}^{f-1}a_{i}
.

Corollary 5.5

For
k\geq 1
, we have

H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))=0.

Proof
.

Consider the short exact sequence of continuous
\Delta_{1}
-modules

0\rightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\varpi}W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\rightarrow A_{\infty}^{\prime}\rightarrow 0,

since Teichmüller lifts give a continuous section of the projection
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\rightarrow A_{\infty}^{\prime}
, Lemma
5.1
and Theorem
5.3
implies that the map

\displaystyle H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))\xrightarrow{\varpi}H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))

(5.6)

is surjective for every
k\geq 1
. For any continuous cocycle
c\in Z^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))
, the surjectivity of (
5.6
) implies that there exists
c_{0}^{\prime}\in C^{k-1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))
and
c_{0}\in Z^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))
such that

c=d^{k-1}(c_{0}^{\prime})+\varpi\cdot c_{0}.

By induction on
n\geq 0
, we can show that for any cocycle
c_{n}\in Z^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))
, there exists
c_{n+1}^{\prime}\in C^{k-1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))
and
c_{n+1}\in Z^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))
such that

c_{n}=d^{k-1}(c_{n+1}^{\prime})+\varpi\cdot c_{n+1},\quad n\geq 0.

It is easy to check that
c^{\prime}\coloneq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\varpi^{i}c_{i}^{\prime}
is a continuous cocycle, and
c=d^{k-1}(c^{\prime})
.
Therefore,

H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))=0,\quad k\geq 1.

5.2
Descent for finite projective modules

The goal of this section is to show that every finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
canonically descends to a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
.

Note that
A_{\infty}
and
A_{\infty}^{\prime}
are isomorphic as perfectoid algebras, hence the topological rings
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
and
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
are isomorphic, therefore, by Lemma
4.1
, every finite projective module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}
is a finite free
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}
-module for
1\leq n\leq+\infty
.

Recall that every element of the ring
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
can be uniquely written as

\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}[x_{i}]\varpi^{i},\quad x_{i}\in A_{\infty}^{\prime}.

Thus for
n\geq 2
, there exists a continuous map of topological spaces

\displaystyle s_{n}:W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n-1}\rightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n},\ \sum_{i=0}^{n-2}[x_{i}]\varpi^{i}\mapstochar\rightarrow\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}[x_{i}]\varpi^{i},

(5.7)

where
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n-1}
and
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}
are endowed with the product topology induced by

\left(A_{\infty}^{\prime}\right)^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n},\ (x_{i})_{0\leq i\leq n-1}\mapstochar\rightarrow\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}[x_{i}]\varpi^{i}.

Lemma 5.6

Let
M
be a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}
of rank
r
, where
n\geq 1
is an integer. Then
M^{\Delta_{1}}
is a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n}
of rank
r
, and there is a natural isomorphism

W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}\otimes_{W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n}}M^{\Delta_{1}}\cong M.

Proof
.

We proceed by induction on
n
. The case
n=1
is Theorem
5.2
. For
n\geq 2
, consider the following short exact sequence of topological
\Delta_{1}
-modules

0\rightarrow\varpi^{n-1}M\rightarrow M\rightarrow M/\varpi^{n-1}\rightarrow 0.

Then
\varpi^{n-1}M
and
M/\varpi^{n-1}
are finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-modules over
A_{\infty}^{\prime}
and
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n-1}
of rank
r
respectively. With a chosen basis of
M
, we can use (
5.7
) to construct a topological section of the quotient map
M\twoheadrightarrow M/\varpi^{n-1}
. Hence we can apply Lemma
5.1
to deduce the following long exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\left(\varpi^{n-1}M\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow M^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow\left(M/\varpi^{n-1}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},\varpi^{n-1}M)\rightarrow\cdots.

(5.8)

Corollary
5.4
ensures that
H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},\varpi^{n-1}M)=0
. By the induction hypothesis,
\left(M/\varpi^{n-1}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
is a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n-1}
. In particular, we can take
e_{1}^{\prime},\dots,e_{r}^{\prime}\in M^{\Delta_{1}}\subset M
such that the image of
e_{1}^{\prime},\dots,e_{r}^{\prime}
in
\left(M/\varpi^{n-1}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
forms a
W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n-1}
-basis of
\left(M/\varpi^{n-1}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
. By the induction hypothesis, the image of
e_{1}^{\prime},\dots,e_{r}^{\prime}
under the surjection
M\twoheadrightarrow M/\varpi^{n-1}
generates
M/\varpi^{n-1}
as a
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n-1}
-module. Hence
M=\sum_{i=1}^{r}W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}\cdot e_{i}^{\prime}+\varpi^{n-1}M.
By Nakayama’s lemma,
e_{1}^{\prime},\dots,e_{r}^{\prime}
generate
M
over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}
. As
M
is a free
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}
-module of rank
r
,
\{e_{1}^{\prime},\dots,e_{r}^{\prime}\}
must be a
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}
-basis of
M
, i.e.
M=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}\cdot e_{i}^{\prime}
. By our choice for
e_{i}^{\prime}
, we have

M^{\Delta_{1}}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\cdot e_{i}^{\prime}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n}\cdot e_{i}^{\prime}.

Thus
M^{\Delta_{1}}
is a free
W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n}
-module of rank
r
. Moreover,
M\cong W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}\otimes_{W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n}}M^{\Delta_{1}}
.

The endomorphism
\varphi_{q}
of
M
(resp.
\varpi^{n-1}M
,
M/\varpi^{n-1}
) commutes with the action of
(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f}
, hence the restriction of
\varphi_{q}
on
M^{\Delta_{1}}\subseteq M
(resp.
\left(\varpi^{n-1}M\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\subseteq\varpi^{n-1}M
,
\left(M/\varpi^{n-1}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\subseteq M/\varpi^{n-1}
) is an endomorphism of
M^{\Delta_{1}}
(resp.
\left(\varpi^{n-1}M\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
,
\left(M/\varpi^{n-1}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
), which makes
M^{\Delta_{1}}
(resp.
\left(\varpi^{n-1}M\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
,
\left(M/\varpi^{n-1}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
) into a
\varphi_{q}
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n}
(resp.
A_{\infty}
,
W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n-1}
).
By the induction hypothesis,
\left(\varpi^{n-1}M\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
and
\left(M/\varpi^{n-1}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
are étale
\varphi_{q}
-modules over
A_{\infty}
and
W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n-1}
respectively, hence are étale over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n}
. Then by a standard argument using five lemma,
M^{\Delta_{1}}
is étale.

Now we are able to prove the following theorem:

Theorem 5.7

The functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M

is an equivalence of categories with a quasi-inverse given by
(-)^{\Delta_{1}}
.

Proof
.

It is easy to check that
\left(-\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\circ\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{W_{E}(A_{\infty})}-\right)\cong\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}))}
. We need to show that for any finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-module
M
over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
of rank
r
,
M^{\Delta_{1}}
is a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
of rank
r
, and there is a canonical isomorphism

W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M^{\Delta_{1}}\cong M.

Since
M
is a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
, we have
M\cong\varprojlim_{n}\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)
,
and
M/\varpi^{n}
is a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n}
of rank
r
for every
n
. Thus by Lemma
5.6
,
\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
is a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n}
of rank
r
. Moreover, there is a natural injection of abelian groups
\varprojlim_{n}\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\hookrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}M/\varpi^{n}\cong M
, and the image of
\varprojlim_{n}\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
is exactly
M^{\Delta_{1}}
. Hence we can identify
\varprojlim_{n}\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
with
M^{\Delta_{1}}
.

The proof of Lemma
5.6
shows that the transition map
\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow\left(M/\varpi^{n-1}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}

is surjective and can be identified with
\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\twoheadrightarrow\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}/\varpi^{n-1}
. Since
\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
is a free
W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n}
-module of rank
r
for every
n\geq 1
, the inverse limit
\varprojlim_{n}\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
is a free
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
-module of rank
r
.

The endomorphism
\varphi_{q}:W_{E}(A_{\infty})\rightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty})
is an isomorphism, hence

\displaystyle W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{\varphi_{q},W_{E}(A_{\infty})}\varprojlim_{n}\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}

\displaystyle\cong\varprojlim_{n}\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty})\otimes_{\varphi_{q},W_{E}(A_{\infty})}\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\cong\varprojlim_{n}\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n}\otimes_{\varphi_{q},W_{E}(A_{\infty})/\varpi^{n}}\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\cong\varprojlim_{n}\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}},

where the last isomorphism uses the étaleness of
\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
for
n\geq 1
.
Hence
\varprojlim_{n}\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}=M^{\Delta_{1}}
is a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
of rank
r
.

It remains to show that the natural injection
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M^{\Delta_{1}}\hookrightarrow M
is an isomorphism. For
m_{1}\in\left(M/\varpi\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
, since every transition map of the inverse system
\left\{\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}:n\geq 1\right\}
is surjective, the natural projection

\mathrm{pr}_{1}:M^{\Delta_{1}}=\varprojlim_{n}\left(M/\varpi^{n}\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow\left(M/\varpi\right)^{\Delta_{1}}

must be surjective.
On the other hand, applying
\left(-\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
to the short exact sequence
0\rightarrow\varpi M\rightarrow M\rightarrow M/\varpi\rightarrow 0
, we get an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\varpi M^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow M^{\Delta_{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}\left(M/\varpi\right)^{\Delta_{1}},

thus the map
\mathrm{pr}_{1}
induces an isomorphism of
A_{\infty}
-modules

M^{\Delta_{1}}/\varpi\cong\left(M/\varpi\right)^{\Delta_{1}}.

By Lemma
5.6
,
A_{\infty}^{\prime}\otimes_{A_{\infty}}\left(M/\varpi\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\cong M/\varpi
, thus the natural map
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M^{\Delta_{1}}\hookrightarrow M
induces an isomorphism

\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M^{\Delta_{1}}\right)/\varpi\cong A_{\infty}^{\prime}\otimes_{A_{\infty}}M^{\Delta_{1}}/\varpi\cong A_{\infty}^{\prime}\otimes_{A_{\infty}}\left(M/\varpi\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\cong M/\varpi.

Since
1+\varpi x
is invertible for every
x\in W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
, we see that
\varpi
is contained in the Jacobson radical of
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
, hence by Nakayama’s lemma, the natural injection
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M^{\Delta_{1}}\hookrightarrow M
is surjective, thus it is an isomorphism.

Corollary 5.8

Let
M
be a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
, then

H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M\right)=0,\quad k\geq 1.

Proof
.

By Theorem
5.7
, as a
\Delta_{1}
-module,
M\cong W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})^{\oplus r}
for some
r\geq 0
. Then the conclusion follows from Corollary
5.5
.

5.3
Descent for finitely presented modules

In this section, we show that the functor
(-)^{\Delta_{1}}
is an equivalence of categories from
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))
to
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}))
with a quasi-inverse given by
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{m,W_{E}(A_{\infty})}-
.

Note that the map

\displaystyle A_{\infty}=\mathbb{F}\left(\negthinspace\left(T_{\sigma_{0}}^{p^{-\infty}}\right)\negthinspace\right)\left\langle\left(\frac{T_{\sigma_{i}}}{T_{\sigma_{0}}}\right)^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}:i\right\rangle

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow A_{\infty}^{\prime}=\mathbb{F}\left(\negthinspace\left(T_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{1/p^{\infty}}\right)\negthinspace\right)\left\langle\left(\frac{T_{\mathrm{LT},i}}{T_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{p^{i}}}\right)^{\pm 1/p^{\infty}}:i\right\rangle

\displaystyle T_{\sigma_{i}}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow T_{\mathrm{LT},i}^{1/p^{i}}

is an isomorphism of perfectoid algebras commuting with
\varphi_{q}
, and it lifts to an isomorphism of topological rings
W_{E}(A_{\infty})\xrightarrow{\sim}W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
commuting with
\varphi_{q}
, hence Proposition
4.21
implies that

Proposition 5.9

Let
M
be a non-zero finitely presented étale
\varphi_{q}
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
. Then there exists
1\leq n_{1}<\dots<n_{r}\leq\infty
and
m_{1},\dots,m_{r}\geq 1
such that

M\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{n_{i}}\right)^{\oplus m_{i}},

here we use the convention that
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})/\varpi^{\infty}=W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
.

As a consequence, we can show that

Proposition 5.10

Let
M
be a finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
. Then

H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\Delta_{1},M)=0,\quad k\geq 1.

Proof
.

If
M
is zero, there is nothing to prove, so we assume that
M
is non-zero. We first deal with torsion modules, i.e. modules
M
such that
\varpi^{n}M=0
and
\varpi^{n-1}M\neq 0
for some
n\geq 1
. We proceed by induction on
n\geq 1
. If
n=1
, Proposition
5.9
implies that
M
is a finite projective étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-module over
A_{\infty}^{\prime}
, then applying Corollary
5.4
, the conclusion follows. For
n\geq 2
, consider the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow M[\varpi^{n-1}]\rightarrow M\rightarrow M/M[\varpi^{n-1}]\rightarrow 0,

By Proposition
5.9
, we can construct a continuous section (of topological spaces)
M/M[\varpi^{n-1}]\rightarrow M
using (
5.7
). Applying Lemma
5.1
, we get a long exact sequence

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle\rightarrow H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)\rightarrow H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M\right)\rightarrow H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M/M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)\rightarrow\cdots.

By the induction hypothesis,

H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)=H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M/M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)=0,

hence for any torsion module
M
,

H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M\right)=0,\quad k\geq 1.

If
M
is torsion-free, Proposition
5.9
implies
M
is finite free, and we apply Corollary
5.8
to conclude.

In general, let
M_{\mathrm{tor}}\coloneq\bigcup_{n=1}^{\infty}M[\varpi^{n}]
be the torsion submodule of
M
which is also finitely presented by Proposition
5.9
, then
M/M_{\mathrm{tor}}
is torsion-free, and there is an exact sequence of finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-modules over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})

0\rightarrow M_{\mathrm{tor}}\rightarrow M\rightarrow M/M_{\mathrm{tor}}\rightarrow 0.

Again, it is easy to see that there is a continuous section of
M\twoheadrightarrow M/M_{\mathrm{tor}}
, hence we may apply Lemma
5.1
to deduce that there is a long exact sequence

\displaystyle\dots\rightarrow H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M_{\mathrm{tor}}\right)\rightarrow H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M\right)\rightarrow H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M/M_{\mathrm{tor}}\right)\rightarrow\dots.

Therefore, for
k\geq 1
,
H^{k}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M\right)=0
.

Theorem 5.11

The functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f}}(W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{W_{E}(A_{\infty})}M

is an equivalence of categories with a quasi-inverse given by
(-)^{\Delta_{1}}
.

Proof
.

Using Proposition
4.21
, one can easily deduce that for every finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module
M
over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
, the natural map
M\rightarrow\left(W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{m}M\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
is an isomorphism.
Let
M
be a finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{f})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
, it remains to show that
M^{\Delta_{1}}
is a finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
, and the natural homomorphism
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{m}M^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow M
is an isomorphism.

If
M
is torsion, suppose that
\varpi^{n-1}M\neq 0
,
\varpi^{n}M=0
, we prove that
M^{\Delta_{1}}
is a finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module by induction on
n\geq 1
. The case
n=1
is Theorem
5.2
. For
n\geq 2
, there is a short exact sequence of finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})
by Proposition
5.9

0\rightarrow M[\varpi^{n-1}]\rightarrow M\rightarrow M/M[\varpi^{n-1}]\rightarrow 0,

and taking
\Delta_{1}
-invariants yields an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\left(M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow M^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow\left(M/M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)

Proposition
5.10
implies that
H^{1}_{\mathrm{cont}}\left(\Delta_{1},M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)=0
, hence there is an exact sequence of
W_{E}(A_{\infty})
-modules

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\left(M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow M^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow\left(M/M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)^{\Delta_{1}}\rightarrow 0.

(5.9)

The induction hypothesis implies that
\left(M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
and
\left(M/M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)^{\Delta_{1}}
are finitely presented, hence
M^{\Delta_{1}}
is also finitely presented. By a standard argument using five lemma,
M^{\Delta_{1}}
is étale. Thus
M^{\Delta_{1}}
is a finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module. Moreover, applying
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{m}-
to the short exact sequence (
5.9
), there is a commutative diagram with exact rows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{m}\left(M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)^{\Delta_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{m}M^{\Delta_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})\otimes_{m}\left(M/M[\varpi^{n-1}]\right)^{\Delta_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M[\varpi^{n-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M/M[\varpi^{n-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}

The left and right vertical maps are isomorphisms by the induction hypothesis, then the snake lemma implies that the middle vertical map is an isomorphism.

If
M
is torsion-free, by Proposition
5.9
,
M
is free. Then the conclusion follows from Theorem
5.7
.

For general
M
, by Proposition
5.9
, there is a short exact sequence of finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-modules over
W_{E}(A_{\infty}^{\prime})

0\rightarrow M_{\mathrm{tor}}\rightarrow M\rightarrow M/M_{\mathrm{tor}}\rightarrow 0.

The same arguments as for torsion representations applies here as well, allowing us to conclude.

6
Review of classical
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules

For a finite
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra
R
with the
p
-adic topology, we denote the category of finite
R
-modules with a continuous
R
-linear
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
-action by
\mathrm{Rep}_{R}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K))
, and let
\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{R}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K))
be the full subcategory consisting of continuous
R
-representations of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
as finite free
R
-modules. In this section, we recall some results on classical étale
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules. The main reference is
[
Fon90
]
,
[
Sch17
]
and
[
CE14
]
.

6.1
Lubin-Tate and cyclotomic
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules

Recall that in Section
3.2
we define the ring
A_{\mathrm{LT},K}
as the
p
-adic completion of
\mathcal{O}_{K}[\negthinspace[T_{\mathrm{LT}}]\negthinspace][\frac{1}{T_{\mathrm{LT}}}]
, and there is a continuous
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-action on
A_{\mathrm{LT},K}
given by (
3.7
).
Let
\left(A_{\mathrm{LT},K}^{\mathrm{ur}}\right)^{\wedge}
be the
p
-adic completion of the maximal unramified extension of
A_{\mathrm{LT},K}
, which admits an action by
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
. The ring
\left(A_{\mathrm{LT},K}^{\mathrm{ur}}\right)^{\wedge}
is a discrete valuation ring with residue field isomorphic to
\mathbb{F}_{q}(\negthinspace(T_{\mathrm{LT}})\negthinspace)^{\mathrm{sep}}
(
[
CE14
, Lemma 6.3]
). Let
\rho
be a finite type
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-representation of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
, the abelian group

D_{\mathrm{LT}}(\rho)\coloneq\left(\left(A_{\mathrm{LT},K}^{\mathrm{ur}}\right)^{\wedge}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\rho\right)^{\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K_{\infty})}

is a finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})
-module over
A_{\mathrm{LT},K}
, and

D_{\mathrm{LT}}:\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K))\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}\left(A_{\mathrm{LT},K}\right)

is an equivalence of categories (see for instance
[
Sch17
, Theorem 3.3.10]
). Moreover, the restriction
D_{\mathrm{LT}}:\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K))\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}\left(A_{\mathrm{LT},K}\right)
is also an equivalence of categories which preserves the rank. As a consequence, we have

Lemma 6.1

The functor

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{LT},\sigma_{0}}:\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}(A_{\mathrm{LT},E,\sigma_{0}})

\displaystyle\rho

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow\left(\left(\mathcal{O}_{E}\otimes_{\sigma_{0},\mathcal{O}_{K}}\left(A_{\mathrm{LT},K}^{\mathrm{ur}}\right)^{\wedge}\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\rho\right)^{\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K_{\infty})}

is an equivalence of categories, and its restriction
D_{\mathrm{LT},\sigma_{0}}:\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K))\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}(A_{\mathrm{LT},E,\sigma_{0}})
is also an equivalence of categories which preserves the rank.

Let
K(\mu_{p^{\infty}})
be the abelian extension of
K
contained in
\overline{K}
generated by all
p^{n}
-th roots of unity for all
n\geq 1
. Since
K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is unramified, we have
\mathrm{Gal}(K(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/K)\cong\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.

Recall that there is a continuous
(\varphi,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-action on
A_{\mathrm{cycl},K}
given by (
3.5
). Let
\left(A_{\mathrm{cycl},K}^{\mathrm{ur}}\right)^{\wedge}
be the
p
-adic completion of the maximal unramified extension of
A_{\mathrm{cycl},K}
, which admits an action by
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
. For
0\leq i\leq f-1
and any embedding
\sigma_{i}:\mathcal{O}_{K}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{E}
, we put
A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{i}}\coloneq\mathcal{O}_{E}\otimes_{\sigma_{i},\mathcal{O}_{K}}A_{\mathrm{cycl},K}
, then

\mathcal{O}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A_{\mathrm{cycl},K}\cong\prod_{i=0}^{f-1}A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{i}},

and the endomorphism
\varphi
of
\mathcal{O}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A_{\mathrm{cycl},K}
induces a homomorphism
\varphi:A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{i}}\rightarrow A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{i-1}}
for every
i
. Let
\varphi_{q}\coloneq\varphi^{f}
be the endomorphism of
A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{i}}
.

For any finite type
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-representation
\rho
of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
and
0\leq i\leq f-1
, by
[
Fon90
]
, the abelian group

D_{\mathrm{cycl},\sigma_{i}}(\rho)\coloneq A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{i}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A_{\mathrm{cycl},K}}\left(\left(A_{\mathrm{cycl},K}^{\mathrm{ur}}\right)^{\wedge}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\rho\right)^{\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K(\mu_{p^{\infty}}))}

is a finitely presented étale
(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-module over
A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{i}}
. In fact, by
[
Fon90
]
, we have

Lemma 6.2

For
0\leq i\leq f-1
, the functor

D_{\mathrm{cycl},\sigma_{i}}:\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K))\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\left(A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{i}}\right)

is an equivalence of categories, and its restriction
D_{\mathrm{cycl},\sigma_{i}}:\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{free}}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K))\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,proj}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\left(A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{i}}\right)
is also an equivalence of categories which preserves the rank.

Since the trivial map
A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{i}}\rightarrow A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{0}},\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{n}T_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{n}\mapstochar\rightarrow\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{n}T_{\mathrm{cycl}}^{n}
is an isomorphism commuting with the continuous
(\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})
-actions for
1\leq i\leq f-1
, we may simply denote
A_{\mathrm{cycl},E,\sigma_{0}}
by
A_{\mathrm{cycl},E}=\left(\mathcal{O}_{E}[\negthinspace[T_{\mathrm{cycl}}]\negthinspace][\frac{1}{T_{\mathrm{cycl}}}]\right)^{\wedge}
where the completion is the
p
-adic completion, and Lemma
6.2
gives an equivalence of categories

D_{\mathrm{cycl},\sigma_{i}}:\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K))\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t,fp}}_{\varphi_{q},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\left(A_{\mathrm{cycl},E}\right)

for every
0\leq i\leq f-1
. Moreover, let
\varphi
be the
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-linear endomorphism of
A_{\mathrm{cycl},E}
which sends
T_{\mathrm{cycl}}
to
(1+T_{\mathrm{cycl}})^{p}-1
, then it is easy to check that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{cycl},\sigma_{0}}(\rho)\cong A_{\mathrm{cycl},E}\otimes_{\varphi^{i},A_{\mathrm{cycl},E}}D_{\mathrm{cycl},\sigma_{i}}(\rho),\quad i=1,\dots,f-1.

(6.1)

6.2
Comparison between
D_{\mathrm{LT},\sigma_{0}}(\rho)
and
